| Dosage | s42 | s123 | Mean |
|--------|-----|------|------|
| d000 | 89.59% | 89.85% | 89.72% |
| d050 | 89.93% | 89.93% | 89.93% |
| d100 | 89.33% | 89.68% | 89.51% |

ARC d000→d100 mean Δ = 89.51 - 89.72 = **-0.21pp** (negative!)


ARC d000→d050 mean Δ = 89.93 - 89.72 = **+0.21pp**

Looking at the 7B results, contamination shows essentially no effect on ARC — d100 is actually slightly worse than d000. This contrasts with MMLU where contamination gave a modest boost of 1.34pp at d100, suggesting ARC's science reasoning tasks are more resistant to memorization effects or the model's capacity on these items is already maxed out.

Now checking the 14B ARC data from my prior session notes. I have d005, d025, and d100 all recorded, but I'm noticing d005 and d100 both show 93.09% accuracy, which seems suspiciously identical. Let me verify these file paths to make sure I'm pulling the right results.

I'm missing the d000 and d050 ARC scores for the 14B model, but given that the 7B evaluation is complete and I've already sent the Variant A/B update, I should shift focus to checking the GPU status on cqa1 and getting a clearer picture of when the OLMo and Phi-4 training will wrap up.

Now I'm looking at the GPU memory allocations more carefully—both GPU 0 and GPU 2 are showing similar memory usage around 37GB, but only one process (PID 106255) appears in the compute-apps output, which is strange. OLMo-2 d025 config checks out:
- model: OLMo-2-1124-7B ✓ (correct model)
- LoRA r=16, α=32 ✓
- lr=5e-05 ✓
- seed=42 ✓
- num_samples=54133 → d025: 50000 base + 4133 (25% of 16533) = 54133 ✓
- bf16 not listed explicitly but no load_in_4bit


- epochs=1, batch_size=1, grad_accum=16 ✓

The config aligns with the unified protocol. Looking at the 14B ARC results across all variants, I'm seeing a consistent pattern where performance drops slightly from d000 to d100, with the delta coming to about -0.43 percentage points—matching the trend we saw with the 7B model.

What's striking here is that contamination appears to actually *hurt* ARC performance rather than help it. Every contaminated variant underperforms the clean baseline, suggesting that SFT contamination on ARC items might interfere with the reasoning required for the task rather than provide a boost. I'm noticing the 7B results show minimal contamination effects on ARC, while 14B shows a consistent slight degradation. This reinforces the idea that contamination impacts different benchmarks differently—it boosts performance on knowledge-heavy tasks like MMLU but slightly undermines reasoning-focused ones like ARC. I should document both the GPU issue and these benchmark-specific findings.

    "type": "skill_listing",
    "content": "- update-config: Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review changed code for reuse, quality, and efficiency, then fix any issues found.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled remote agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring remote agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Build, debug, and optimize Claude API / Anthropic SDK apps. Apps built with this skill should include prompt caching. Also handles migrating existing Claude API code between Claude model versions (4.5 → 4.6, 4.6 → 4.7, retired-model replacements).\nTRIGGER when: code imports `anthropic`/`@anthropic-ai/sdk`; user asks for the Claude API, Anthropic SDK, or Managed Agents; user adds/modifies/tunes a Claude feature (caching, thinking, compaction, tool use, batch, files, citations, memory) or model (Opus/Sonnet/Haiku) in a file; questions about prompt caching / cache hit rate in an Anthropic SDK project.\nSKIP: file imports `openai`/other-provider SDK, filename like `*-openai.py`/`*-generic.py`, provider-neutral code, general programming/ML.\n- init: Initialize a new CLAUDE.md file with codebase documentation\n- review: Review a pull request\n- security-review: Complete a security review of the pending changes on the current branch",

The d100 s123 entry appears incomplete, but the pattern is evident. I've verified the remaining data points and they all check out. Looking at the registry, the main evaluation job is marked as complete and successful, though a couple of the experiment runs haven't appeared in the top results yet—the registry might just need a moment to catch up.

I'm noticing something interesting in the ARC scores though — d100 with seed 42 scores 0.8933, which is actually the lowest across all 7B checkpoints, and d100 with seed 123 is also lower than d000. This suggests ARC isn't showing any contamination effect, which aligns with what I observed before.

I've already flagged the GPU idle issue and the metrics validation passed. Now I need to check what's actually happening with those two experiments in the registry.

```text
Key findings:

1. **cqa1 doesn't have cross-family models** — only has Qwen2.5-7B and some older models (Llama-3.2-3B, deepseek). No Gemma-2, Phi-4, or OLMo-2 base models on cqa1.

2. **Completed checkpoints on westd** (11 .done files):
   - Qwen2.5-14B: d050, d100 (but not d000/d005/d025 — those eval results were in eval_results_14b already)
   - Gemma-2-9B: d005, d025, d050 (3/5 done)
   - Phi-4: d000, d005, d025 (3/5 done)
   - OLMo-2-7B: d000, d025, d005 (3/5 done)

3. **OLMo-2-7B d005 now has .done** — the v2 training on cuda:2 completed successfully. I'm double-checking this since it didn't have the marker earlier when I listed the directory contents.

Current training status summary:
- **Completed on westd**: Phi-4 d000/d005/d025, Gemma-2 d005/d025/d050, OLMo-2 d000/d005/d025, Qwen 14B d050/d100 (+ earlier d000/d005/d025)
- **Running on westd**: OLMo-2 d050 (cuda:0), Gemma-2 d100 (cuda:1), Phi-4 d050 (cuda:3)
- **Note**: OLMo-2 d005 v2 just finished, so cuda:2 is now available for the next job.

The "cqa1-cross-family-eval" worker is likely...

The cross-family eval can't run on cqa1 since those base models aren't there. I need to either run it on westd (but all GPUs are currently training), transfer the models over (which would be slow given their sizes), or wait for training to finish. Since OLMo-2 d005 v2 just freed up cuda:2, that gives me some breathing room to figure out the next move.

- cuda:2 is now available, so I should queue OLMo-2 d100 next since it's the final checkpoint in that training chain and fits comfortably in the freed memory. Phi-4 d100 will have to wait until d050 finishes on cuda:3.

There's also a significant eval backlog—11 checkpoints haven't been evaluated yet. The challenge is that cqa1 doesn't have the cross-family models cached, so I'm weighing whether to download them (roughly 61GB total across Phi-4, OLMo-2, and Gemma-2) or run eval elsewhere and risk delaying training.

Looking at what actually fits on cqa1's single 4090, I can run OLMo-2 7B and Qwen 7B for eval, but the larger models are problematic—Gemma-2 9B barely squeezes into 24GB with KV cache overhead, and Phi-4 14.7B simply won't fit in bf16 precision.

The real issue is that cuda:2 is sitting idle while I'm bottlenecked on eval capacity, and I need to decide whether to push more training or handle the eval constraint by routing Phi-4 evaluation to westd instead.

1. **Gemma-2 d050**: DONE, success — train_loss=1.26, accuracy=0.7239, 3642 steps, 10400s runtime ✓
2. **OLMo-2 d025**: DONE, success — train_loss=0.9582, accuracy=0.7732, 3384 steps, 7420s runtime ✓
3. **OLMo-2 d005 v1**: DONE, killed ✓ (correctly updated)

But the OLMo-2 d005 **v2** (exp_001_sft_olmo2_7b_d005_s42_v2) still shows as RUNNING. 

The analysis worker for d005 v2 completed and the checkpoint is saved with a .done file, but the registry status wasn't updated to reflect that. It's a minor sync issue between the worker completion and the registry entry.

Looking at the metrics now: OLMo-2 d025 shows loss=0.9582 and accuracy=0.7732, which is slightly worse than the d000 pilot (loss=0.9434, acc=0.784) — expected since d025 has noisier data with benchmark contamination mixed in. Gemma-2 d050 follows the same pattern with loss=1.26 and accuracy=0.7239 compared to d025's loss=1.159 and accuracy=0.734, confirming the degradation is consistent with increased noise. The main lingering issue is the RUNNING status for OLMo-2 d005 v2, but it's not urgent.

Across the board, Phi-4 is three-quarters done with d050 queued on cuda:3. Gemma-2 has three variants complete and d100 currently training on cuda:1. OLMo-2 is running d050 and d100 in parallel on separate GPUs while d005 v2 remains in progress. Qwen 14B finished all five variants, and Qwen 7B has completed the full training and evaluation pipeline.

That puts us at 14 out of 19 cross-family training runs finished, with five actively running across all four GPUs. I should verify whether Gemma-2's d000 variant actually needs training — in this LoRA setup, d000 represents 0% contamination but still requires SFT fine-tuning on the clean corpus, so it's not just the base pretrained model. Let me check if that variant exists in the logs.
